OPENUCX(1)                                                                                        OpenUCX                                                                                        OPENUCX(1)

NAME
     openucx - OpenUCX

     Unified Communication X (UCX) is an award winning <https://losalamosreporter.com/2019/11/07/nine-los-alamos-national-laboratory-projects-win-rd-100-awards>, optimized production-proven communication
     framework for modern, high-bandwidth and low-latency networks.

     UCX  exposes  a  set  of abstract communication primitives that utilize the best of available hardware resources and offloads. These include RDMA (InfiniBand and RoCE), TCP, GPUs, shared memory, and
     network atomic operations.

     UCX facilitates rapid development by providing a high-level API, masking the low-level details, while maintaining high-performance and scalability.

     UCX implements best practices for transfer of messages of all sizes, based on accumulated experience gained from applications running on the world's largest datacenters and supercomputers. The  full
     list of features and capabilities can be found here <#ucx-features>.

     UCX is a member of UCF consortium <https://www.ucfconsortium.org>.  [image: UCX layer diagram] [image]

UCX MAIN FEATURES
   High-level API features
     • Select either a client/server connection establishment (similar to TCP), or connect directly by passing remote address blob.

     • Support sharing resources between threads, or allocating dedicated resources per thread.

     • Event-driven or polling-driven progress.

     • Java and Python bindings.

     • Seamless handling of GPU memory.

   Main APIs
     • Stream-oriented send/receive operations.

     • Tag-matched send/receive.

     • Remote memory access.

     • Remote atomic operations.

   Fabrics support
     • RoCE

     • InfiniBand

     • TCP sockets

     • Shared memory (CMA, knem, xpmem, SysV, mmap)

     • Cray Gemini / Aries (ugni)

     • NVLink / Multi-Node NVLink

   Platforms support
     • Supported architectures: x86_64, Arm v8, Power.

     • Runs on virtual machines (using SRIOV) and containers (docker, singularity).

     • Can utilize either MLNX_OFED or Inbox RDMA drivers.

     • Tested on major Linux distributions (RedHat/Ubuntu/SLES).

   GPU support
     • Cuda (for NVIDIA GPUs)

     • ROCm (for AMD GPUs)

   Protocols, Optimizations and Advanced Features
     • Automatic selection of best transports and devices.

     • Zero-copy with registration cache.

     • Scalable flow control algorithms.

     • Optimized memory pools.

     • Accelerated direct-verbs transport for Mellanox devices.

     • Pipeline protocols for GPU memory

     • QoS and traffic isolation for RDMA transports

     • Platform (micro-architecture) specific optimizations (such as memcpy, memory barriers, etc.)

     • Multi-rail and RoCE link aggregation group support

     • Bare-metal, containers and cloud environments support

     • Advanced protocols for transfer messages of different sizes

RUNNING UCX
   UCX build and install
   Getting the source
     • Download latest code from github:

        $ git clone https://github.com/openucx/ucx.git ucx
        $ cd ucx
        $ ./autogen.sh

     • Alternatively, download and extract one of UCX pre-configured releases <>:

        $ wget https://github.com/openucx/ucx/releases/download/v1.22.0/ucx-1.22.0.tar.gz
        $ tar xzf ucx-1.22.0.tar.gz
        $ cd ucx-1.22.0

     • (This step is only required for OpenPOWER platforms) On Ubuntu platform the config.guess file is a bit outdated and does not have support for power. In order to resolve the issue you have to down‐
       load an updated config.guess.  From the root of the project:

          $ wget https://github.com/shamisp/ucx/raw/topic/power8-config/config.guess

   Building
     • Configure:

        $ mkdir build
        $ cd build
        $ ../configure --prefix=<ucx-install-path>
        NOTE: For best performance configuration, use ../contrib/configure-release instead of ../configure.  This will strip all debugging and profiling code.

     • Build and install:

        $ make -j4
        $ make install

                                                                                                      ----

   OpenMPI with UCX
     OpenMPI <https://www.open-mpi.org> supports UCX starting from version 3.0, but it's recommended to use version 4.0 or higher due to stability and performance improvements.

   Building
     • Get latest-and-greatest OpenMPI version:

        $ git clone --recurse-submodules  https://github.com/open-mpi/ompi.git
        $ cd ompi
        $ ./autogen.pl

     • Configure with UCX:

        $ mkdir build-ucx
        $ cd build-ucx
        $ ../configure --prefix=<ompi-install-path> --with-ucx=<ucx-install-path>
        NOTE: With OpenMPI 4.0 and above, there could be compilation errors from "btl_uct" component.  This component is not critical for using UCX; so it could be disabled this way:

            $ ./configure ... --enable-mca-no-build=btl-uct ...

     • Build:

        $ make
        $ make install

   Running MPI
     Example of the command line (with optional flag to select IB device mlx5_0 port 1):

        $ mpirun -np 2 -mca pml ucx -x UCX_NET_DEVICES=mlx5_0:1 ./app
        IMPORTANT  NOTE:  Recent  OpenMPI  versions  contain a BTL component called 'uct', which can cause data corruption when enabled, due to conflict on malloc hooks between OPAL and UCM.  In order to
        work-around this, use one of the following alternatives:

        Alternative 1: Disable btl/uct in OpenMPI build configuration:

            $ ./configure ... --enable-mca-no-build=btl-uct ...

        Alternative 2: Disable btl/uct at runtime

            $ mpirun -np 2 -mca pml ucx -mca btl ^uct -x UCX_NET_DEVICES=mlx5_0:1 ./app

   Runtime tunings
     By default OpenMPI enables built-in transports (BTLs), which may result in additional software overheads in the OpenMPI progress function. In order to workaround this issue you may  try  to  disable
     certain BTLs.

        $ mpirun -np 2 -mca pml ucx --mca btl ^vader,tcp,openib,uct -x UCX_NET_DEVICES=mlx5_0:1 ./app

                                                                                                      ----

   MPICH with UCX
     UCX is supported in MPICH 3.3 and higher versions.  UCX is already embedded in the MPICH tarball, so you do not need to separately download UCX.

   Building
     • Download mpich-3.3 or higher from https://www.mpich.org

     • Configure with UCX:

        $ mkdir build
        $ cd build
        $ ../configure --prefix=<mpich-install-path> --with-device=ch4:ucx

     • Build MPICH:

        $ make -j4
        $ make install

   Running MPI
     Example of the command line (with optional flag to select IB device mlx5_0 port 1):

        $ mpirun -np 2 -env UCX_NET_DEVICES=mlx5_0:1 ./executable

   Running in Docker containers
     UCX can run in a container, but requires slight adjustments:

     • Some transports may be unsupported, depending on the runtime configuration.  To see the available transports use ucx_info -d.

     • In  order  to  enable  shared  memory  transport,  ptrace  capability  is required. The recommended shared memory size is 8GB. Add the following to docker run commandline: --cap-add CAP_SYS_PTRACE
       --shm-size="8g".

     • To use the shared memory between several containers, add this instead: --cap-add CAP_SYS_PTRACE --ipc host.

     • To enable RDMA:

       • One of: rdma-core, libibverbs or MLNX_OFED packages has to be installed.

       • The corresponding devices must be allowed: --device=/dev/infiniband/rdma_cm --device=/dev/infiniband/uverbsX (replace X with the device number).

       • ulimit -l should be set to a value large enough to accommodate for transport resources and send/receive buffers. At least 128MB is recommended, a higher value may be needed  if  the  application
         working set is large.

   Application Context routines
     group UCP Application Context
            Application context is a primary concept of UCP design which provides an isolation mechanism, allowing resources associated with the context to separate or share network communication context
            across multiple instances of applications.

            This section provides a detailed description of this concept and routines associated with it.

            Defines

            UCP_ENTITY_NAME_MAX
                   Maximum size of the UCP entity name in structure of entity attributes provided by a query method.

            Typedefs

            typedef struct ucp_lib_attr <#_CPPv412ucp_lib_attr> ucp_lib_attr_t
                   Lib attributes.

                   The structure defines the attributes that characterize the Library.

            typedef struct ucp_context_attr <#_CPPv416ucp_context_attr> ucp_context_attr_t
                   Context attributes.

                   The structure defines the attributes that characterize the particular context.

            typedef struct ucp_tag_recv_info <#_CPPv417ucp_tag_recv_info> ucp_tag_recv_info_t
                   UCP receive information descriptor.

                   The   UCP   receive   information   descriptor   is   allocated   by   application   and   filled   in   with  the  information  about  the  received  message  by  ucp_tag_probe_nb  <#
                   group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga41668f468dc37a7634116b8210815f22> or ucp_tag_recv_request_test <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga707cbbef8cdcf90fa7cf63c922ef2c7f> routines or  ucp_tag_recv_call‐
                   back_t <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga00ac0fe5665d15fab5cd254af9b07758> callback argument.

            typedef struct ucp_context *ucp_context_h
                   UCP Application Context.

                   UCP  application  context  (or  just a context) is an opaque handle that holds a UCP communication instance's global information. It represents a single UCP communication instance. The
                   communication instance could be an OS process (an application) that uses UCP library. This global information includes communication resources, endpoints, memory, temporary file  stor‐
                   age,  and other communication information directly associated with a specific UCP instance. The context also acts as an isolation mechanism, allowing resources associated with the con‐
                   text to manage multiple concurrent communication instances. For example, users using both MPI and OpenSHMEM sessions simultaneously can isolate their communication  by  allocating  and
                   using separate contexts for each of them. Alternatively, users can share the communication resources (memory, network resource context, etc.) between them by using the same application
                   context. A message sent or a RMA operation performed in one application context cannot be received in any other application context.

            typedef void (*ucp_request_init_callback_t)(void *request)
                   Request initialization callback.

                   This callback routine is responsible for the request initialization.

                   Param request
                          [in] Request handle to initialize.

            typedef void (*ucp_request_cleanup_callback_t)(void *request)
                   Request cleanup callback.

                   This callback routine is responsible for cleanup of the memory associated with the request.

                   Param request
                          [in] Request handle to cleanup.

            static inline ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_init(const ucp_params_t <#_CPPv412ucp_params_t> *params, const ucp_config_t <#_CPPv412ucp_config_t> *config, ucp_context_h <#
            _CPPv413ucp_context_h> *context_p)
                   UCP context initialization.

                   This routine creates and initializes a UCP application context <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>.

                   This routine checks API version compatibility, then discovers the available network interfaces, and initializes the network resources required for discovering of the network and memory
                   related devices. This routine is responsible for initialization all information required for a particular application scope, for example, MPI application, OpenSHMEM application, etc.

                   Parameters

                          • config -- [in] UCP configuration descriptor allocated through ucp_config_read() <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_f_i_g_1gab2a5bd39a60f5326771c7a46f5f93319> routine.

                          • params   --   [in]   User   defined   ucp_params_t  <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_f_i_g_1ga91aad075a1692f3bea2df3c59e63ed5c>  configurations  for  the  UCP  application  context  <#
                            group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>.

                          • context_p -- [out] Initialized UCP application context <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>.

                   Returns
                          Error code as defined by ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558>

                   Warning:
                      This routine must be called before any other UCP function call in the application.

                   Note:

                      • Higher level protocols can add additional communication isolation, as MPI does with its communicator object. A single communication context may be used  to  support  multiple  MPI
                        communicators.

                      • The context can be used to isolate the communication that corresponds to different protocols. For example, if MPI and OpenSHMEM are using UCP to isolate the MPI communication from
                        the OpenSHMEM communication, users should use different application context for each of the communication libraries.

            void ucp_cleanup(ucp_context_h <#_CPPv413ucp_context_h> context_p)
                   Release UCP application context.

                   This routine finalizes and releases the resources associated with a UCP application context <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>.

                   The  cleanup  process releases and shuts down all resources associated with the application context. After calling this routine, calling any UCP routine without calling UCP initializa‐
                   tion routine <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga3ad3662ebafac88ec666be4caeb76cb2> is invalid.

                   Parameters
                          context_p -- [in] Handle to UCP application context <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>.

                   Warning:
                      An application cannot call any UCP routine once the UCP application context released.

            struct ucp_request_param_t
                   #include <ucp.h>

                   Operation       parameters        passed        to        ucp_tag_send_nbx        <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga8323878b60f426c630d4ff8996ede3cc>,        ucp_tag_send_sync_nbx        <#

                   The  structure  ucp_request_param_t  <#structucp__request__param__t> is used to specify datatype of operation, provide user request in case the external request is used, set completion
                   callback and custom user data passed to this callback.

                   Example: implementation of function to send contiguous buffer to ep and invoke callback function at operation completion. If the operation completed immediately (status == UCS_OK) then
                   callback is not called.

                      ucs_status_ptr_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1gaf4381d25f2fb0cf02f996f3e0729ed62> send_data(ucp_ep_h <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> ep, void *buffer, size_t length,
                                                 ucp_tag_t <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga55df42689ef1f5621eae4d1ffb16856e> tag, void *request <#structucp__request__param__t_1a746646123a460ed9a115c3fe21f194b0>)
                      {
                          ucp_request_param_t param = {
                              .op_attr_mask               = UCP_OP_ATTR_FIELD_CALLBACK <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gga67fae646dd1668efba6efe49a35a6610a73e3a6e8b96b37ec1b0764e67dd13602> |
                                                            UCP_OP_ATTR_FIELD_REQUEST <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gga67fae646dd1668efba6efe49a35a6610acfac7dfc3e7b5967870f5376c334b996>,
                              .request                    = request <#structucp__request__param__t_1a746646123a460ed9a115c3fe21f194b0>,
                              .cb.send                    = custom_send_callback_f,
                              .user_data                  = pointer_to_user_context_passed_to_cb
                          };

                          ucs_status_ptr_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1gaf4381d25f2fb0cf02f996f3e0729ed62> status;

                          status = ucp_tag_send_nbx <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga8323878b60f426c630d4ff8996ede3cc>(ep, buffer, length, tag, &param);
                          if (UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(status)) {
                              handle_error(status);
                          } else if (status == UCS_OK <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1gga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558a1a7fbae02ac33fb94f519c7c773f419a>) {
                              // operation is completed
                          }

                          return status;
                      }

            ucp_request_param_t.cb
                   Callback function that is invoked whenever the send or receive operation is completed.

                   Public Members

                   ucp_send_nbx_callback_t <#_CPPv423ucp_send_nbx_callback_t> send

                   ucp_tag_recv_nbx_callback_t <#_CPPv427ucp_tag_recv_nbx_callback_t> recv

            typedef struct ucp_address ucp_address_t
                   UCP worker address.

                   The address handle is an opaque object that is used as an identifier for a worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f> instance.

            typedef struct ucp_listener *ucp_listener_h
                   UCP listen handle.

                   The listener handle is an opaque object that is used for listening on a specific address and accepting connections from clients.

            typedef struct ucp_worker *ucp_worker_h
                   UCP Worker.

                   UCP  worker  is an opaque object representing the communication context. The worker represents an instance of a local communication resource and the progress engine associated with it.
                   The progress engine is a construct that is responsible for asynchronous and independent progress of communication directives. The progress engine could be implemented  in  hardware  or
                   software. The worker object abstracts an instance of network resources such as a host channel adapter port, network interface, or multiple resources such as multiple network interfaces
                   or  communication ports. It could also represent virtual communication resources that are defined across multiple devices. Although the worker can represent multiple network resources,
                   it is associated with a single UCX application context <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>. All communication functions require a  context  to  perform
                   the operation on the dedicated hardware resource(s) and an endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> to address the destination.

                   Note:
                      Worker are parallel "threading points" that an upper layer may use to optimize concurrent communications.

            typedef void (*ucp_listener_accept_callback_t)(ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> ep, void *arg)
                   A callback for accepting client/server connections on a listener ucp_listener_h <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gac0496f7102c43cf339cc760b9e5724a2>.

                   This callback routine is invoked on the server side upon creating a connection to a remote client. The user can pass an argument to this callback. The user is responsible for releasing
                   the ep handle using the ucp_ep_destroy() <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gac63b3fe87c001dd965ca42943ea04bb1> routine.

                   Param ep
                          [in] Handle to a newly created endpoint which is connected to the remote peer which has initiated the connection.

                   Param arg
                          [in] User's argument for the callback.

            typedef void (*ucp_listener_conn_callback_t)(ucp_conn_request_h <#_CPPv418ucp_conn_request_h> conn_request, void *arg)
                   A callback for handling of incoming connection request conn_request from a client.

                   This  callback  routine is invoked on the server side to handle incoming connections from remote clients. The user can pass an argument to this callback. The conn_request handle has to
                   be       released,       either       by       ucp_ep_create       <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga6cc5ffb2ba1b0ccd510848de0a779f7b>        or        ucp_listener_reject        <#
                   group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga1f1620fdbae11ea076875fd6ac644241> routine.

                   Param conn_request
                          [in] Connection request handle.

                   Param arg
                          [in] User's argument for the callback.

            typedef struct ucp_listener_conn_handler <#_CPPv425ucp_listener_conn_handler> ucp_listener_conn_handler_t
                   UCP callback to handle the connection request in a client-server connection establishment flow.

                   This  structure is used for handling an incoming connection request on the listener. Setting this type of handler allows creating an endpoint on any other worker and not limited to the
                   worker on which the listener was created.

                   Note:

                      • Other than communication progress routines, it is allowed to call all other communication routines from the callback in the struct.

                      • The callback is thread safe with respect to the worker it is invoked on.

                      • It is the user's responsibility to avoid potential dead lock accessing different worker.

            typedef enum ucp_wakeup_event_types <#_CPPv422ucp_wakeup_event_types> ucp_wakeup_event_t
                   UCP worker wakeup events mask.

                   The  enumeration  allows  specifying  which  events  are   expected   on   wakeup.   Empty   events   are   possible   for   any   type   of   event   except   for   UCP_WAKEUP_TX   <#
                   group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gga66be8946dcd33051d40ceca65ba28e22ad70899effbabed3294c35b9b0f2a747b>                       and                       UCP_WAKEUP_RX                      <#
                   group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gga66be8946dcd33051d40ceca65ba28e22a182a353c533fc335e638e41fdaf7c6b5>.

                   Note:
                      Send completions are reported by POLLIN-like events (see poll man page). Since outgoing operations can be initiated at any time, UCP does not generate POLLOUT-like events,  although
                      it must be noted that outgoing operations may be queued depending upon resource availability.

            enum ucp_am_cb_flags
                   Flags for a UCP Active Message callback.

                   Flags that indicate how to handle UCP Active Messages.

                   Values:

                   enumerator UCP_AM_FLAG_WHOLE_MSG
                          Indicates that the entire message will be handled in one callback.

                   enumerator UCP_AM_FLAG_PERSISTENT_DATA
                          Guarantees  that  the  specified  ucp_am_recv_callback_t  <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gab3adc96222b05543c6cba8c28c16703b>   callback,   will   always   be   called   with
                          UCP_AM_RECV_ATTR_FLAG_DATA  <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gga487a3c588a11e42b9f313f0c9e2ead3baf77ecce2987e81bf773a48363767b77e> flag set, so the data will be accessible outside the
                          callback, until ucp_am_data_release <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga45570903fc3ce01e7e5c0cd241f516f9> is called.

            enum ucp_send_am_flags
                   Flags for sending a UCP Active Message.

                   Flags       dictate       the       behavior       of       ucp_am_send_nb       <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga94ec21811930b6b6fb8eab500b8c730d>       and       ucp_am_send_nbx       <#
                   group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga4b8ab214f35828ee4608d73442e8c372> routines.

                   Values:

                   enumerator UCP_AM_SEND_FLAG_REPLY
                          Force relevant reply endpoint to be passed to the data callback on the receiver.

                   enumerator UCP_AM_SEND_FLAG_EAGER
                          Force UCP to use only eager protocol for AM sends.

                   enumerator UCP_AM_SEND_FLAG_RNDV
                          Force UCP to use only rendezvous protocol for AM sends.

                   enumerator UCP_AM_SEND_FLAG_COPY_HEADER
                          The  flag  indicates that the header should be copied to an internal buffer in case it's needed after the send function returns. If this flag is specified, the header can be re‐
                          leased immediately after the send function returns, even if the non-blocking send request is not completed.

                   enumerator UCP_AM_SEND_REPLY
                          Backward compatibility.

            enum ucp_wakeup_event_types
                   UCP worker wakeup events mask.

                   The  enumeration  allows  specifying  which  events  are   expected   on   wakeup.   Empty   events   are   possible   for   any   type   of   event   except   for   UCP_WAKEUP_TX   <#
                   group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gga66be8946dcd33051d40ceca65ba28e22ad70899effbabed3294c35b9b0f2a747b>                       and                       UCP_WAKEUP_RX                      <#
                   group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gga66be8946dcd33051d40ceca65ba28e22a182a353c533fc335e638e41fdaf7c6b5>.

                   Note:
                      Send completions are reported by POLLIN-like events (see poll man page). Since outgoing operations can be initiated at any time, UCP does not generate POLLOUT-like events,  although
                      it must be noted that outgoing operations may be queued depending upon resource availability.

                   Values:

                   enumerator UCP_WAKEUP_RMA
                          Remote memory access send completion

                   enumerator UCP_WAKEUP_AMO
                          Atomic operation send completion

                   enumerator UCP_WAKEUP_TAG_SEND
                          Tag send completion

                   enumerator UCP_WAKEUP_TAG_RECV
                          Tag receive completion

                   enumerator UCP_WAKEUP_TX
                          This  event  type  will  generate  an  event  on completion of any outgoing operation (complete or partial, according to the underlying protocol) for any type of transfer (send,
                          atomic, or RMA).

                   enumerator UCP_WAKEUP_RX
                          This event type will generate an event on completion of any receive operation (complete or partial, according to the underlying protocol).

                   enumerator UCP_WAKEUP_EDGE
                          Use edge-triggered wakeup. The event file descriptor will be signaled only for new events, rather than existing ones.

            Functions

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_worker_create(ucp_context_h <#_CPPv413ucp_context_h> context, const ucp_worker_params_t <#_CPPv419ucp_worker_params_t> *params, ucp_worker_h <#
            _CPPv412ucp_worker_h> *worker_p)
                   Create a worker object.

                   This routine allocates and initializes a worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f> object. Each worker is associated with one and only  one  application
                   <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>    context.    In    the    same    time,    an    application    context    can   create   multiple   workers   <#
                   group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f> in order to enable concurrent access to communication resources. For example,  application  can  allocate  a  dedicated
                   worker for each application thread, where every worker can be progressed independently of others.

                   Parameters

                          • context -- [in] Handle to UCP application context <#group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>.

                          • params   --   [in]   User   defined   ucp_worker_params_t   <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga06d7269d208e74bbf09fda6b4d3a7109>   configurations   for   the   UCP   worker   <#
                            group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>.

                          • worker_p -- [out] A pointer to the worker object allocated by the UCP library

                   Returns
                          Error code as defined by ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558>

                   Note:
                      The worker object is allocated within context of the calling thread

            void ucp_worker_destroy(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker)
                   Destroy a worker object.

                   This routine releases the resources associated with a UCP worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>.

                   The destroy process releases and shuts down all resources associated with the worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>.

                   Parameters
                          worker -- [in] Worker object to destroy.

                   Warning:
                      Once the UCP worker destroy the worker handle cannot be used with any UCP routine.

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_worker_address_query(ucp_address_t <#_CPPv413ucp_address_t> *address, ucp_worker_address_attr_t <#_CPPv425ucp_worker_address_attr_t> *attr)

            unsigned ucp_worker_progress(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker)
                   Progress all communications on a specific worker.

                   This routine explicitly progresses all communication operations on a worker.

                   Parameters
                          worker -- [in] Worker to progress.

                   Returns
                          Non-zero if any communication was progressed, zero otherwise.

                   Note:

                      • Typically, request wait and test routines call this routine <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga340784a8528d4932916651460dc481c0> to progress any outstanding operations.

                      • Transport layers, implementing asynchronous progress using threads, require callbacks and other user code to be thread safe.

                      • The state of communication can be advanced (progressed) by blocking routines. Nevertheless, the non-blocking routines can not be used for communication progress.

            ssize_t ucp_stream_worker_poll(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker, ucp_stream_poll_ep_t <#_CPPv420ucp_stream_poll_ep_t> *poll_eps, size_t max_eps, unsigned flags)
                   Poll for endpoints that are ready to consume streaming data.

                   This non-blocking routine returns endpoints on a worker which are ready to consume streaming data. The ready endpoints are placed in poll_eps array, and the function return value indi‐
                   cates how many are there.

                   Parameters

                          • worker -- [in] Worker to poll.

                          • poll_eps -- [out] Pointer to array of endpoints, should be allocated by user.

                          • max_eps -- [in] Maximum number of endpoints that should be filled in poll_eps.

                          • flags -- [in] Reserved for future use.

                   Returns
                          Negative value indicates an error according to ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558>. On success, non-negative value (less or equal
                          max_eps) indicates actual number of endpoints filled in poll_eps array.

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_listener_create(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker, const ucp_listener_params_t <#_CPPv421ucp_listener_params_t> *params, ucp_listener_h <#
            _CPPv414ucp_listener_h> *listener_p)
                   Create a listener to accept connections on. Connection requests on the listener will arrive at a local address specified by the user.

                   This  routine  creates a new listener object that is bound to a specific local address. The listener will listen to incoming connection requests. After receiving a request from the re‐
                   mote peer, an endpoint to this peer will be created - either right away or by calling ucp_ep_create <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga6cc5ffb2ba1b0ccd510848de0a779f7b>, as specified
                   by the callback type in ucp_listener_params_t <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga440ab104a3ed59271948a28d6eb1f224>. The user's callback will be invoked once the endpoint is created.

                   Parameters

                          • worker -- [in] Worker object to create the listener on.

                          • params  --  [in]  User  defined   ucp_listener_params_t   <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga440ab104a3ed59271948a28d6eb1f224>   configurations   for   the   ucp_listener_h   <#

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_worker_set_am_recv_handler(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker, const ucp_am_handler_param_t <#_CPPv422ucp_am_handler_param_t> *param)

                   This routine installs a user defined callback to handle incoming Active Messages with a specific id. This callback is called whenever an Active Message that was sent  from  the  remote

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_worker_fence(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker)
                   Assures ordering between non-blocking operations.

                   This  routine  ensures  ordering of non-blocking communication operations on the UCP worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>. Communication operations
                   issued on a particular endpoint created on the worker prior to this call are guaranteed to be completed before any communication operations issued on the same endpoint after this call.

                   Parameters
                          worker -- [in] UCP worker.

                   Returns
                          Error code as defined by ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558>

                   Note:
                      The    primary    difference    between    ucp_worker_fence()     <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga05b3b5887475966631c62119d64c6182>     and     the     ucp_worker_flush_nb()     <#
                      group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gac461d1034a3313612243a5829b7e3847>  is  the  fact the fence routine does not guarantee completion of the operations on the call return but only ensures
                      the order between communication operations. The flush <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gac461d1034a3313612243a5829b7e3847> operation on return guarantees that all operations are  com‐
                      pleted and corresponding memory regions were updated.

            ucs_status_ptr_t <#_CPPv416ucs_status_ptr_t> ucp_worker_flush_nbx(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker, const ucp_request_param_t <#_CPPv419ucp_request_param_t> *param)
                   Flush outstanding AMO and RMA operations on the worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>.

                   This  routine flushes all outstanding AMO and RMA communications on the worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>. All the AMO and RMA operations issued
                   on the worker prior to this call are completed both at the origin and at the target when this call returns.

                   Parameters

                          • worker -- [in] UCP worker.

                          • param -- [in] Operation parameters, see ucp_request_param_t <#structucp__request__param__t>

                   Returns
                          NULL - The flush operation was completed immediately.

                   Returns
                          UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(_ptr) - The flush operation failed.

                   Returns
                          otherwise - Flush operation was scheduled and can be completed in any point in time. The request handle is returned to the application in order to track progress.

                   Note:
                      For      description      of      the      differences      between      flush      <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gac461d1034a3313612243a5829b7e3847>       and       fence       <#
                      group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga05b3b5887475966631c62119d64c6182> operations please see ucp_worker_fence() <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga05b3b5887475966631c62119d64c6182>

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_worker_flush(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker)
                   Flush outstanding AMO and RMA operations on the worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>.

                   Deprecated:
                          Replaced   by   ucp_worker_flush_nb   <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gac461d1034a3313612243a5829b7e3847>.   The   following  example  implements  the  same  functionality  using
                          ucp_worker_flush_nb <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gac461d1034a3313612243a5829b7e3847> :

                      ucs_status_t worker_flush(ucp_worker_h worker)
                      {
                          void *request = ucp_worker_flush_nb(worker);
                          if (request == NULL) {
                              return UCS_OK;
                          } else if (UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(request)) {
                              return UCS_PTR_STATUS(request);
                          } else {
                              ucs_status_t status;
                              do {
                                  ucp_worker_progress(worker);
                                  status = ucp_request_check_status(request);
                              } while (status == UCS_INPROGRESS);
                              ucp_request_release(request);
                              return status;
                          }
                      }

                   This routine flushes all outstanding AMO and RMA communications on the worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>. All the AMO and RMA operations  issued
                   on the worker prior to this call are completed both at the origin and at the target when this call returns.

                   Parameters
                          worker -- [in] UCP worker.

                   Returns
                          Error code as defined by ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558>

                   Note:
                      For       description       of       the      differences      between      flush      <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gae20b8bc9fbbde208ea266490bde4cbcb>      and      fence      <#
                      group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga05b3b5887475966631c62119d64c6182> operations please see ucp_worker_fence() <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga05b3b5887475966631c62119d64c6182>

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_worker_get_address(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker, ucp_address_t <#_CPPv413ucp_address_t> **address_p, size_t *address_length_p)
                   Get the address of the worker object.

            struct ucp_listener_conn_handler
                   #include <ucp_def.h>

                   UCP callback to handle the connection request in a client-server connection establishment flow.

                   This structure is used for handling an incoming connection request on the listener. Setting this type of handler allows creating an endpoint on any other worker and not limited to  the
                   worker on which the listener was created.

                   Note:

                      • Other than communication progress routines, it is allowed to call all other communication routines from the callback in the struct.

                      • The callback is thread safe with respect to the worker it is invoked on.

                      • It is the user's responsibility to avoid potential dead lock accessing different worker.

            typedef struct ucp_ep *ucp_ep_h
                   UCP Endpoint.

                   The endpoint handle is an opaque object that is used to address a remote worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>. It typically provides a  description
                   of  source,  destination,  or  both.  All  UCP  communication  routines  address  a destination with the endpoint handle. The endpoint handle is associated with only one UCP context <#
                   group___u_c_p___c_o_n_t_e_x_t_1ga0e40ddc46f0bbe868a13f6ab1b674f76>. UCP provides the endpoint create <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga6cc5ffb2ba1b0ccd510848de0a779f7b>  routine  to
                   create the endpoint handle and the destroy <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gac63b3fe87c001dd965ca42943ea04bb1> routine to destroy the endpoint handle.

            typedef struct ucp_conn_request *ucp_conn_request_h
                   UCP connection request.

                   A server-side handle to incoming connection request. Can be used to create an endpoint which connects back to the client.

            typedef ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> (*ucp_am_callback_t)(void *arg, void *data, size_t length, ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> reply_ep, unsigned flags)
                   Callback to process incoming Active Message.

                   When the callback is called, flags indicates how data should be handled.

                   Param arg
                          [in] User-defined argument.

                   Param data
                          [in]    Points    to    the   received   data.   This   data   may   persist   after   the   callback   returns   and   needs   to   be   freed   with   ucp_am_data_release   <#
                          group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga45570903fc3ce01e7e5c0cd241f516f9>.

                   Param length
                          [in] Length of data.

                   Param reply_ep
                          [in] If the Active Message is sent with the UCP_AM_SEND_FLAG_REPLY flag, the sending ep will be passed in. If not, NULL will be passed.

                   Param flags
                          [in] If this flag is set to UCP_CB_PARAM_FLAG_DATA, the callback can return UCS_INPROGRESS and data will persist after the callback returns.

                   Return
                          UCS_OK data will not persist after the callback returns.

                   Return
                          UCS_INPROGRESS Can only be returned if flags is set to UCP_CB_PARAM_FLAG_DATA. If UCP_INPROGRESS is returned, data will persist after the callback has returned. To free the mem‐
                          ory, a pointer to the data must be passed into ucp_am_data_release <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga45570903fc3ce01e7e5c0cd241f516f9>.

                   Note:
                      This callback should be set and released by ucp_worker_set_am_handler <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gab8e272749e7a396643caf8775889df4e> function.

            typedef ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> (*ucp_am_recv_callback_t)(void *arg, const void *header, size_t header_length, void *data, size_t length, const ucp_am_recv_param_t <#
            _CPPv419ucp_am_recv_param_t> *param)
                   Callback to process incoming Active Message sent by ucp_am_send_nbx <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga4b8ab214f35828ee4608d73442e8c372> routine.

                   The callback is always called from the progress context, therefore calling ucp_worker_progress() <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga340784a8528d4932916651460dc481c0> is not  allowed.  It
                   is recommended to define callbacks with relatively short execution time to avoid blocking of communication progress.

                   Param arg
                          [in] User-defined argument.

                   Param header
                          [in] User defined active message header. If header_length is 0, this value is undefined and must not be accessed.

                   Param header_length
                          [in] Active message header length in bytes.

                   Param data
                          [in]      Points      to      the      received      data      if     UCP_AM_RECV_ATTR_FLAG_RNDV     flag     is     not     set     in     ucp_am_recv_param_t::recv_attr     <#
                          structucp__am__recv__param_1a53a8777a31faf7e65289bcc1d710fb19>. Otherwise it points to the internal UCP descriptor which can further be used for initiating data receive by using
                          ucp_am_recv_data_nbx <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gaefaaa5e0a154efe496ae5bb7f2bf71f3> routine.

                   Param length
                          [in] Length of data. If UCP_AM_RECV_ATTR_FLAG_RNDV flag is set in ucp_am_recv_param_t::recv_attr <#structucp__am__recv__param_1a53a8777a31faf7e65289bcc1d710fb19>,  it  indicates
                          the required receive buffer size for initiating rendezvous protocol.

                   Param param
                          [in] Data receive parameters.

                   Return
                          UCS_OK   data   will   not   persist   after   the   callback   returns.   If   UCP_AM_RECV_ATTR_FLAG_RNDV   flag   is   set  in  param->recv_attr  and  ucp_am_recv_data_nbx  <#
                          group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gaefaaa5e0a154efe496ae5bb7f2bf71f3> was not called for this data, the  data  descriptor  will  be  dropped  and  the  corresponding  ucp_am_send_nbx  <#
                          group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga4b8ab214f35828ee4608d73442e8c372> call will complete with UCS_OK status.

                   Return
                          UCS_INPROGRESS Can only be returned if param->recv_attr flags contains UCP_AM_RECV_ATTR_FLAG_DATA or UCP_AM_RECV_ATTR_FLAG_RNDV. The data will persist after the callback has re‐
                          turned.  To free the memory, a pointer to the data must be passed into ucp_am_data_release <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga45570903fc3ce01e7e5c0cd241f516f9> or data receive is ini‐
                          tiated by ucp_am_recv_data_nbx <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gaefaaa5e0a154efe496ae5bb7f2bf71f3>.

                   Return
                          otherwise Can only be returned if param->recv_attr contains UCP_AM_RECV_ATTR_FLAG_RNDV. In this case data descriptor data will be dropped and the  corresponding  ucp_am_send_nbx
                          <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga4b8ab214f35828ee4608d73442e8c372> call on the sender side will complete with the status returned from the callback.

                   Note:
                      This callback should be set and released by ucp_worker_set_am_recv_handler <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga410d5e90c2abc6997924cbf7dee4a1f3> function.

            enum ucp_err_handling_mode_t
                   Error handling mode for the UCP endpoint.

                   Specifies error handling mode for the UCP endpoint.

                   Values:

                   enumerator UCP_ERR_HANDLING_MODE_NONE
                          No guarantees about error reporting, imposes minimal overhead from a performance perspective.

                          Note:
                             In this mode, any error reporting will not generate calls to ucp_ep_params_t::err_handler <#structucp__ep__params_1aaf329de9e2b45311099cee8c9df43a20>.

                   enumerator UCP_ERR_HANDLING_MODE_PEER
                          Guarantees that send requests are always completed (successfully or error) even in case of remote failure, disables protocols and APIs which may cause a hang or undefined behav‐
                          ior in case of peer failure, may affect performance and memory footprint

                   enumerator UCP_ERR_HANDLING_MODE_FAILOVER
                          In case of error on transport layer, protocol layer tries to handle the error by best effort and continue operation by using the next available communication channel. If the er‐
                          ror cannot be handled, see UCP_ERR_HANDLING_MODE_PEER <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gga7c69a28724d5ae3e49490e23e64df167aa31630259732e700b6ce0fe612cf0a6f>.

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_ep_create(ucp_worker_h <#_CPPv412ucp_worker_h> worker, const ucp_ep_params_t <#_CPPv415ucp_ep_params_t> *params, ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> *ep_p)
                   Create and connect an endpoint.

                   This    routine     creates     and     connects     an     endpoint     <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>     on     a     local     worker     <#
                   group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f> for a destination address <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga7d24d2759c1375bf4d2d639e3b701956> that identifies the remote
                   worker  <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>.  This function is non-blocking, and communications may begin immediately after it returns. If the connection
                   process is not completed, communications may be delayed. The created endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> is associated with one and only one
                   worker <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f>.

                   Parameters

                          • worker -- [in] Handle to the worker; the endpoint is associated with the worker.

                          • params  --  [in]   User   defined   ucp_ep_params_t   <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gae071db9736cd54afebe6f619401c6617>   configurations   for   the   UCP   endpoint   <#
                            group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>.

                          • ep_p -- [out] A handle to the created endpoint.

                   Returns
                          Error code as defined by ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558>

                   Note:
                      One of the following fields has to be specified:.INDENT 0.0

                      • ucp_ep_params_t::address <#structucp__ep__params_1a8cf5c1599022ccdea5a6d1c97ced3627>

                      • ucp_ep_params_t::sockaddr <#structucp__ep__params_1aa23fd9a6a092266371258bd285c96816>

                      • ucp_ep_params_t::conn_request <#structucp__ep__params_1a9fbba62cd4a470f2bc7c6f7e55fe3db1>

            Note:
               By  default,  ucp_ep_create()  <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga6cc5ffb2ba1b0ccd510848de0a779f7b>  will  connect an endpoint to itself if the endpoint is destined to the same worker on
               which   it   was   created,   i.e.   params.address    belongs    to    worker.    This    behavior    can    be    changed    by    passing    the    UCP_EP_PARAMS_FLAGS_NO_LOOPBACK    <#
               group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gga4a1f422dc1e81495b4aee1da8939459fa519d4e4a7237ae543f72ddeaef6864ec>  flag  in params.flags. In that case, the endpoint will be connected to the next end‐
               point created in the same way on the same worker.

            ucs_status_ptr_t <#_CPPv416ucs_status_ptr_t> ucp_ep_close_nbx(ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> ep, const ucp_request_param_t <#_CPPv419ucp_request_param_t> *param)
                   Non-blocking endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> closure.

                   Parameters

                          • ep -- [in] Handle to the endpoint to close.

                          • param -- [in] Operation parameters, see ucp_request_param_t <#structucp__request__param__t>. This operation supports specific flags, which can be passed in  param  by  ucp_re‐
                            quest_param_t::flags    <#structucp__request__param__t_1a55a8ff2559470c5def6077a86ee9d898>.    The    exact    set   of   flags   is   defined   by   ucp_ep_close_flags_t   <#
                            group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gacef728a5ee7a11f50794544aa259fe71>.

                   Returns
                          NULL - The endpoint is closed successfully.

                   Returns
                          UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(_ptr) - The closure failed and an error code indicates the transport level status. However, resources are released and the endpoint can no longer be used.

                   Returns
                          otherwise - The closure process is started, and can be completed at any point in time. A request handle is returned to the application in order to track progress of the endpoint
                          closure.

            ucs_status_ptr_t <#_CPPv416ucs_status_ptr_t> ucp_ep_flush_nbx(ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> ep, const ucp_request_param_t <#_CPPv419ucp_request_param_t> *param)
                   Non-blocking flush of outstanding AMO and RMA operations on the endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>.

                   This routine flushes all outstanding AMO and RMA communications on the endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>. All the AMO and  RMA  operations
                   issued  on  the  ep prior to this call are completed both at the origin and at the target endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> when this call
                   returns.

                   The following example demonstrates how blocking flush can be implemented using non-blocking flush: .INDENT 7.0

               ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558> blocking_ep_flush(ucp_ep_h <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> ep, ucp_worker_h <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga5fd52efba31d20fc52055bb19270729f> worker)
               {
                   ucp_request_param_t <#structucp__request__param__t> param;
                   void *request;

                   param.op_attr_mask <#structucp__request__param__t_1a97e9444548efb351db6a07130fb69c28> = 0;
                   request            = ucp_ep_flush_nbx <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga28d61bf53320bd0f5d741147447251a4>(ep, &param);
                   if (request == NULL) {
                       return UCS_OK <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1gga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558a1a7fbae02ac33fb94f519c7c773f419a>;
                   } else if (UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(request)) {
                       return UCS_PTR_STATUS(request);
                   } else {
                       ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558> status;
                       do {
                           ucp_worker_progress <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga340784a8528d4932916651460dc481c0>(worker);
                           status = ucp_request_check_status <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gae082ad7af428645ebe6e469d3d06a757>(request);
                       } while (status == UCS_INPROGRESS <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1gga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558a77aa02620851779729e4ad6ceb41f84a>);
                       ucp_request_free <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga0e8e46f5953d464382b21edef3ec9994>(request);
                       return status;
                   }
               }

            Parameters

                   • ep -- [in] UCP endpoint.

                   • param -- [in] Operation parameters, see ucp_request_param_t <#structucp__request__param__t>.

            Returns
                   NULL - The flush operation was completed immediately.

            Returns
                   UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(_ptr) - The flush operation failed.

            Returns
                   otherwise - Flush operation was scheduled and can be completed in any point in time. The request handle is returned to the application in order to track progress.

            ucs_status_ptr_t <#_CPPv416ucs_status_ptr_t> ucp_ep_modify_nb(ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> ep, const ucp_ep_params_t <#_CPPv415ucp_ep_params_t> *params)
                   Modify endpoint parameters.

                   Deprecated:
                          Use      ucp_listener_conn_handler_t       <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1gaec337bd9fcab44386c01f76699010b63>       instead       of       ucp_listener_accept_handler_t       <#
                          group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga6b4950841b36dfddd268fcb2cce2a52c>,  if you have other use case please submit an issue on <https://github.com/openucx/ucx> or report to <ucx-group@
                          elist.ornl.gov>

                   This       routine        modifies        endpoint        <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>        created        by        ucp_ep_create        <#
                   group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga6cc5ffb2ba1b0ccd510848de0a779f7b>  or ucp_listener_accept_callback_t <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga9a1d61f9a3c1fd97c008dd77ae86c02c>. For example,
                   this API can be used  to  setup  custom  parameters  like  ucp_ep_params_t::user_data  <#structucp__ep__params_1adaf49e16ee05400f715f432e442336cb>  or  ucp_ep_params_t::err_handler  <#
                   structucp__ep__params_1aaf329de9e2b45311099cee8c9df43a20> to endpoint created by ucp_listener_accept_callback_t <#group___u_c_p___w_o_r_k_e_r_1ga9a1d61f9a3c1fd97c008dd77ae86c02c>.

                   Parameters

                          • ep -- [in] A handle to the endpoint.

                          • params   --   [in]   User   defined   ucp_ep_params_t   <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gae071db9736cd54afebe6f619401c6617>   configurations   for   the   UCP  endpoint  <#
                            group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>.

                   Returns
                          NULL - The endpoint is modified successfully.

                   Returns
                          UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(_ptr) - The reconfiguration failed and an error code indicates the status. However, the endpoint is not modified and can be used further.

                   Returns
                          otherwise - The reconfiguration process is started, and can be completed at any point in time. A request handle is returned to the application in order to track progress of  the
                          endpoint modification. The application is responsible for releasing the handle using the ucp_request_free <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga0e8e46f5953d464382b21edef3ec9994> routine.

                   Note:
                      See the documentation of ucp_ep_params_t <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gae071db9736cd54afebe6f619401c6617> for details, only some of the parameters can be modified.

            ucs_status_ptr_t <#_CPPv416ucs_status_ptr_t> ucp_ep_close_nb(ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> ep, unsigned mode)
                   Non-blocking endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> closure.

                   Deprecated:
                          Use ucp_ep_close_nbx <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga6756b434213a424abb0d542fda1d82a1> instead.

                   This routine releases the endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>. The endpoint closure process depends on the selected mode.

                   Parameters

                          • ep -- [in] Handle to the endpoint to close.

                          • mode -- [in] One from ucp_ep_close_mode <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga3be4aa93d76e41c80c780965f2f8ae57> value.

                   Returns
                          UCS_OK - The endpoint is closed successfully.

                   Returns
                          UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(_ptr) - The closure failed and an error code indicates the transport level status. However, resources are released and the endpoint can no longer be used.

                   Returns
                          otherwise - The closure process is started, and can be completed at any point in time. A request handle is returned to the application in order to track progress of the endpoint
                          closure. The application is responsible for releasing the handle using the ucp_request_free <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga0e8e46f5953d464382b21edef3ec9994> routine.

                   Note:
                      ucp_ep_close_nb            <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gaa7293ad4dc72858b2594686d1c553015>            replaces            deprecated            ucp_disconnect_nb           <#
                      group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga22cf67f74ae06242cc26e6ca6ecd100b> and ucp_ep_destroy <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gac63b3fe87c001dd965ca42943ea04bb1>

            ucs_status_ptr_t <#_CPPv416ucs_status_ptr_t> ucp_ep_flush_nb(ucp_ep_h <#_CPPv48ucp_ep_h> ep, unsigned flags, ucp_send_callback_t <#_CPPv419ucp_send_callback_t> cb)
                   Non-blocking flush of outstanding AMO and RMA operations on the endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>.

                   Deprecated:
                          Use ucp_ep_flush_nbx <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga28d61bf53320bd0f5d741147447251a4> instead.

                   This routine flushes all outstanding AMO and RMA communications on the endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf>. All the AMO and  RMA  operations
                   issued  on  the  ep prior to this call are completed both at the origin and at the target endpoint <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga9bea4e4174ea25f44285d8e7a9fb7adf> when this call
                   returns.

                   Parameters

                          • ep -- [in] UCP endpoint.

                          • flags -- [in] Flags for flush operation. Reserved for future use.

                          • cb -- [in] Callback which will be called when the flush operation completes.

                   Returns
                          NULL - The flush operation was completed immediately.

                   Returns
                          UCS_PTR_IS_ERR(_ptr) - The flush operation failed.

                   Returns
                          otherwise - Flush operation was scheduled and can be completed in any point in time. The request handle is returned to the application in order to track progress.  The  applica‐
                          tion is responsible for releasing the handle using ucp_request_free() <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga0e8e46f5953d464382b21edef3ec9994> routine.

            struct ucp_transport_entry_t
                   #include <ucp_def.h>

                   The     ucp_transports_t     <#structucp__transports__t>    and    ucp_transport_entry_t    <#structucp__transport__entry__t>    structures    are    used    when    ucp_ep_query    <#
                   group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga5074a508e2ae53e00d2bd7e728336f30> is called to return an array of transport name and device name pairs that are used by an active endpoint.

                   The ucp_transport_t structure specifies the characteristics of the ucp_transport_entry_t <#structucp__transport__entry__t> array.

                   The caller is responsible for the allocation and de-allocation of the ucp_transport_entry_t <#structucp__transport__entry__t> array.

                   Example: Implementation of a function to query the set of transport and device name pairs used by the specified endpoint.

                        ucp_ep_attr_t <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1gaf870daeabc3b91d521d9063eb1b1027d> ep_attrs;

                        ep_attrs.field_mask <#structucp__ep__attr_1a385c4a4337266da2a874e5ff533fbab2> = UCP_EP_ATTR_FIELD_TRANSPORTS <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ggaa24832a718990ceb558ab5a1d4abfc41a0eeddc2e29662e9ab046ac862757d11e>;
                        ep_attrs.transports <#structucp__ep__attr_1a2104226ca6c263e31b669a148185181b>.entries <#structucp__transports__t_1a4603a2147c2627572889484eeed7a32e> = (ucp_transport_entry_t *)
                                 malloc(10 * sizeof(ucp_transport_entry_t));
                        ep_attrs.transports <#structucp__ep__attr_1a2104226ca6c263e31b669a148185181b>.num_entries <#structucp__transports__t_1a21eabc3b6df3b0c6964de56ab7784304> = 10;
                        ep_attrs.transports <#structucp__ep__attr_1a2104226ca6c263e31b669a148185181b>.entry_size <#structucp__transports__t_1a0f09e5a459259f70ee7644f62daa2adc> = sizeof(ucp_transport_entry_t);
                        status = ucp_ep_query <#group___u_c_p___e_n_d_p_o_i_n_t_1ga5074a508e2ae53e00d2bd7e728336f30>(ep, &ep_attrs);
                        if (status == UCS_OK <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1gga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558a1a7fbae02ac33fb94f519c7c773f419a>) {
                            // ep_attrs.transports.num_entries = number of returned entries

                   A transport name and device name pair used by this endpoint. The caller is responsible for the allocation and deallocation of an array of these structures large enough to  contain  the

            ucp_dt_make_contig(_elem_size)
                   Generate an identifier for contiguous data type.

                   This macro creates an identifier for contiguous datatype that is defined by the size of the basic element.

                   Parameters

                   Returns

                   Note:

              structucp__request__param__t_1a224d06cf5fc852537393f0653b421f6a> specifies the number of remote elements.

     Typedefs

            typedef struct ucp_dt_iov <#_CPPv410ucp_dt_iov> ucp_dt_iov_t
                   Structure for scatter-gather I/O.

                   This structure is used to specify a list of buffers which can be used within a single data transfer function call. This list should remain valid until the data transfer request is com‐
                   pleted.

                   Note:
                      If length is zero, the memory pointed to by buffer will not be accessed. Otherwise, buffer must point to valid memory.

            typedef struct ucp_generic_dt_ops <#_CPPv418ucp_generic_dt_ops> ucp_generic_dt_ops_t
                   UCP generic data type descriptor.

                   This structure provides a generic datatype descriptor that is used for definition of application defined datatypes.

                   Typically, the descriptor is used for an integration with datatype engines implemented within MPI and SHMEM implementations.

                   Note:
                      In case of partial receive, any amount of received data is acceptable which matches buffer size.

                   This structure provides attributes of a UCP datatype.

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> ucp_dt_create_generic(const ucp_generic_dt_ops_t <#_CPPv420ucp_generic_dt_ops_t> *ops, void *context, ucp_datatype_t <#_CPPv414ucp_datatype_t>
            *datatype_p)
                   Create a generic datatype.

                   This routine create a generic datatype object. The generic datatype is described by the ops object <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1gabece4d5eeeb7f7f22b03dd2fc00e1fdd> which provides
                   a table of routines defining the operations for generic datatype manipulation. Typically, generic datatypes are used for integration with datatype engines provided with MPI implementa‐
                   tions     (MPICH,     Open     MPI,     etc).     The     application     is     responsible     for     releasing     the     datatype_p     object     using    ucp_dt_destroy()    <#
                   group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga00337159ebd75640d55be501b7fcc5e7> routine.

                   Parameters

                          • ops -- [in] Generic datatype function table as defined by ucp_generic_dt_ops_t <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1gabece4d5eeeb7f7f22b03dd2fc00e1fdd> .

                          • context -- [in] Application defined context passed to this routine. The context is passed as a parameter to the routines in the ops table.

                          • datatype_p -- [out] A pointer to datatype object.

                   Returns
                          Error code as defined by ucs_status_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558>

            void ucp_dt_destroy(ucp_datatype_t <#_CPPv414ucp_datatype_t> datatype)
                   Destroy a datatype and release its resources.

                   This routine destroys the datatype object and releases any resources that are associated with the object. The  datatype  object  must  be  allocated  using  ucp_dt_create_generic()  <#
                   group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1gad786aa1a5250b22d4c35d82801aef254> routine.

                   Parameters
                          datatype -- [in] Datatype object to destroy.

                   Warning:

                      • Once the datatype object is released an access to this object may cause an undefined failure.

            void *(*start_pack)(void *context, const void *buffer, size_t count)
                   Start a packing request.

                   The pointer refers to application defined start-to-pack routine. It will be called from the ucp_tag_send_nb <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gaae7343bd638924e5518041311d5c1dfc> routine.

                   Param context
                          [in] User-defined context.

                   Param buffer
                          [in] Buffer to pack.

                   Param count
                          [in] Number of elements to pack into the buffer.

                   Return
                          A custom state that is passed to the following pack() <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga347b6c1b7243047f3e069a70626a1e89> routine.

            void *(*start_unpack)(void *context, void *buffer, size_t count)
                   Start an unpacking request.

                   The pointer refers to application defined start-to-unpack routine. It will be called from the ucp_tag_recv_nb <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1gaeb24cdfc33dfb2886551b51843aa6304> routine.

                   Param context
                          [in] User-defined context.

                   Param buffer
                          [in] Buffer to unpack to.

                   Param count
                          [in] Number of elements to unpack in the buffer.

                   Return
                          A custom state that is passed later to the following unpack() <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1gaef4bcfb34731b7f113479b221304b698> routine.

            size_t (*packed_size)(void *state)
                   Get the total size of packed data.

                   The pointer refers to user defined routine that returns the size of data in a packed format.

                   Param state
                          [in] State as returned by start_pack() <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga80d7580ef18274885656e4ab226e7e96> routine.

                   Return
                          The size of the data in a packed form.

            size_t (*pack)(void *state, size_t offset, void *dest, size_t max_length)
                   Pack data.

                   The pointer refers to application defined pack routine.

                   Param state
                          [in] State as returned by start_pack() <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga80d7580ef18274885656e4ab226e7e96> routine.

                   Param offset
                          [in] Virtual offset in the output stream.

                   Param dest
                          [in] Destination buffer to pack the data.

                   Param max_length
                          [in] Maximum length to pack.

                   Return
                          The size of the data that was written to the destination buffer. Must be less than or equal to max_length.

            ucs_status_t <#_CPPv412ucs_status_t> (*unpack)(void *state, size_t offset, const void *src, size_t length)
                   Unpack data.

                   The pointer refers to application defined unpack routine.

                   Param state
                          [in] State as returned by start_unpack() <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga32dc370dd9d442d48409c5ab2518e937> routine.

                   Param offset
                          [in] Virtual offset in the input stream.

                   Param src
                          [in] Source to unpack the data from.

                   Param length
                          [in] Length to unpack.

                   Return
                          UCS_OK or an error if unpacking failed.

            void (*finish)(void *state)
                   Finish packing/unpacking.

                   The pointer refers to application defined finish routine.

                   Param state
                          [in]       State      as      returned      by      start_pack()      <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga80d7580ef18274885656e4ab226e7e96>      and      start_unpack()      <#
                          group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga32dc370dd9d442d48409c5ab2518e937> routines.

            struct ucp_dt_iov
                   #include <ucp.h>

                   Structure for scatter-gather I/O.

                   This structure is used to specify a list of buffers which can be used within a single data transfer function call. This list should remain valid until the data transfer request is com‐
                   pleted.

                   Note:
                      If length is zero, the memory pointed to by buffer will not be accessed. Otherwise, buffer must point to valid memory.

            struct ucp_dt_local_sgl_t
                   #include <ucp.h>

                   Local SGL descriptor for multi-element operations.

                   This structure describes per-element local buffers, lengths, and memory handles. Element i describes a local buffer at buffers[i] of lengths[i] bytes with memory handle memhs[i].

                   Optionally, the counts and strides arrays describe a repeating pattern for each element: element i represents counts[i] blocks of lengths[i] bytes, each separated by strides[i]  bytes,
                   starting at buffers[i]. When counts and strides are not set, each element is transferred once (equivalent to count=1, stride=0).

                   The  descriptor  ucp_dt_local_sgl_t  <#structucp__dt__local__sgl__t>  itself is copied by the library, so the caller may release it after the call returns. However, the arrays buffers,
                   lengths, memhs, counts, and strides are not copied and must remain valid until the data transfer request is completed.

                   Pass     as     the     buffer     parameter     to     ucp_put_nbx     <#group___u_c_p___c_o_m_m_1ga29e7b9b70b782140e63544b8613a5fe7>     with     ucp_request_param_t::datatype     <#
                   structucp__request__param__t_1a3988d603154f90eb5405785cac62b65f> set to ucp_dt_make_sgl() <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga9cd816ea6437de7670ae54c07b87800b>.

                   Note:
                      Currently only N->N mapping is supported: both sides must use the SGL datatype with equal counts and matching lengths.

                   Note:
                      All  buffers  in the descriptor must share the same memory type (see ucs_memory_type_t <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1ga0da130a6b3524f3f7642b76f698903c7>), otherwise UCS_ERR_IN‐
                      VALID_PARAM <#group___u_c_s___r_e_s_o_u_r_c_e_1gga88ca72d7294772e7d2edb70a2df15558ab5a5c5278c83f957a096444de28db597> is returned.

            struct ucp_dt_remote_sgl_t
                   #include <ucp.h>

                   Remote SGL descriptor for multi-element operations.

                   This structure describes per-element remote addresses, lengths, and keys. Element i targets remote address remote_addrs[i] using key rkeys[i].

                   The descriptor ucp_dt_remote_sgl_t <#structucp__dt__remote__sgl__t> itself is copied by the library, so the caller may release it after the call returns. However, the arrays remote_ad‐
                   drs, lengths, and rkeys are not copied and must remain valid until the data transfer request is completed.

                   Pass       via       ucp_request_param_t::remote       <#structucp__request__param__t_1a31c50bf8fb64c510906f0875cd67ccda>       with       ucp_request_param_t::remote_datatype       <#
                   structucp__request__param__t_1a3eec6861d8aab69ba0c7821d4cbb8caf> set to ucp_dt_make_sgl() <#group___u_c_p___d_a_t_a_t_y_p_e_1ga9cd816ea6437de7670ae54c07b87800b>.

            struct ucp_generic_dt_ops
                   #include <ucp.h>

                   UCP generic data type descriptor.

                   This structure provides a generic datatype descriptor that is used for definition of application defined datatypes.

                   Typically, the descriptor is used for an integration with datatype engines implemented within MPI and SHMEM implementations.

                   Note:
                      In case of partial receive, any amount of received data is acceptable which matches buffer size.

            struct ucp_datatype_attr
                   #include <ucp.h>

                   UCP datatype attributes.

                   This structure provides attributes of a UCP datatype.
